Final answer could be:

```
Theorem (Greenfield--Wallach). Let M be compact connected and X∈C∞(M,TM). The following are equivalent:
(i) X is globally hypoelliptic: Xu∈C∞ ⇒ u∈C∞ for u∈D'(M).
(ii) There exist a smooth diffeomorphism h:M→T^d, a vector α∈R^d satisfying a Diophantine condition, and a smooth positive function ρ on T^d such that h_*X = ρ R_α. Moreover, if α is Diophantine then any such smooth time change ρ R_α is smoothly conjugate to R_α; in particular h can be chosen so that h_*X = R_α.
```

Proposition 2.2 (minimality): Flow minimal.
Proof: Let K minimal compact invariant (exists by Zorn). As distribution L_Xν=0, so ν=ρ m with ρ∈C∞. Since ν probability, ρ not zero; choose p with ρ(p)>0; by continuity U={ρ>0} open nonempty, U⊂suppν⊂K. Proposition 2.3: Unique invariant probability measure with smooth positive density.

Theorem 3.1 (Greenfield–Wallach). Let X be a smooth vector field on compact connected M satisfying (GH). Then X:C∞(M)→C∞(M) has closed range. Moreover, if μ is the unique invariant probability from Proposition 2.3, then
